A run of zero tests now fails with `no test ran`, the summary always counts tests, and the node and vitest reporters set the exit status of their process when the summary fails. `packages/template-ts/tests/cli.test.ts` skipped its 3 tests when `packages/template-ts/dist` was absent; it now builds the package with `scripts/build-package.mjs` in a hook without a time limit, and on a checkout without `dist` its 3 tests pass after the build. `make lint` ran gofmt, `cargo fmt --check` of the Rust crate and the PHP extension and pint only when their package directory existed, and `make docs-check` ran `scripts/check-doc-coverage.mjs` only when the file existed; the recipes run them unconditionally, and gofmt fails when it cannot run. `selectLanguages` of `tests/runner/drivers.mjs` dropped a language whose package directory was absent without a message; it returns every language, and `prepare` fails on the absent package. The new `tests/scripts/no-silent-skips.test.mjs` failed in its 3 cases on the tree of T19.3 and passes after the change; the new case of `tests/scripts/run-tests.test.mjs` failed before the change and its 16 cases pass after it; `make lint` and `make test-ts` (790 tests) pass.
- Created every temporary directory and generated test of the checks and runners under the system temporary directory, never in the checkout (T20.1). Nine runners and scripts made their temporary directory with `mkdtemp` in the repository root (`.generated-*`, `.tmp-generated-program-*`), the Go module or the Rust crate, five made a Go package directory in `packages/template-go`, and six wrote a Rust integration test into `packages/template-rust/tests`: the generated code imports `@polyspec/template`, the module of `packages/template-go` and the crate of `packages/template-rust`, which resolved from those places. The test files of one `node --test` run start these checks at once, so `tests/scripts/owner-check.test.mjs` failed in 4 of 4 runs of `make test-scripts` on the files of `.generated-conformance-ts-*`, and a concurrent `cargo test` of the crate could compile a half-written test. `scripts/temporary-workspace.mjs` now makes the workspaces in the system temporary directory: an ES module directory whose `node_modules` holds a copy of the installed `@polyspec/template`; a crate that depends on `packages/template-rust` by path, whose `Cargo.lock` starts as a copy of the crate's lock and fails when cargo chooses another package, version or checksum, and whose tests build into the target of `packages/template-rust` with `--locked --offline`; and a Go module that requires the module of `packages/template-go` and replaces it with that directory. `tests/scripts/temporary-files.test.mjs` fails, naming file and line, on an `mkdtemp` in the checkout, a directory for generated code in the checkout and a generated integration test in a crate; it listed the 20 places before the change and passes after it. After the change three runs of `make test-scripts` passed with 111 tests, `make generated-native-check typed-generator-compile-check conformance-all-modes` passed, with 248 of 248 generated cases in each of TypeScript, Go, Rust and PHP, and `git status --untracked-files=all` showed no new file.
- Kept the directory of `tests/scripts/process-group.test.mjs` small (T20.3-1). Its three writers created nested directories without bound, and under the load of `make test-scripts` the 20 removals took longer than the 30 s that a test has by default; the test failed with `test timed out after 30000ms` in 1 of 3 runs. The writers now create directories among 50 names each, and the test has its own time limit of 300 s; it passes in about 2.5 s, and a helper that does not wait for the group still fails it with `ENOTEMPTY` in 3 of 3 runs.
- Printed the compiler errors of a Go package that does not build (T19.3). `go test -json` reports the compiler output as `build-output` events and the end of the build as a `build-fail` event; `goEvents` of `scripts/run-tests.mjs` dropped both, so for a module whose test called an undefined function the run printed only `✖ example.com/broken` and `0 passed, 1 failed, the tool exited with 1`, without the line `./broken_test.go:5:33: undefined: missing`. The runner now prints each compiler line and `✖ build of <package> failed`, and the summary line adds `build errors:` with the first 10 compiler errors. The new case of `tests/scripts/run-tests.test.mjs` failed before the change and its 15 cases pass after it.
- Ran every recipe with the toolchains that the checkout declares (T19.2). After T19.1 set `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` for the Go install project, `make install-check` failed on a machine with Go 1.27.0, because `packages/template-go/go.mod` declares 1.27.1; the npm of the recipes was the npm of the machine, and the PHP and Composer of the CI jobs were the latest releases of `8.5`, `8.2` and `v2`. `make install-tools` (`scripts/install-tools.mjs`) installs the npm of the new `packageManager` field of package.json, 12.2.0, with the npm of the machine into `var/tools/npm`, and downloads Go 1.27.1 as the module `golang.org/toolchain@v0.0.1-go1.27.1.<os>-<arch>` with the Go of the machine into `var/tools/go`, making the commands of `bin` and `pkg/tool` executable because the module zip keeps no file modes; it writes wrapper scripts, no symbolic link, into `var/tools/bin`, starts the bootstrap npm and Go without `var/tools/bin` on PATH and skips an install of a present version. `make install` depends on it. The Makefile puts `var/tools/bin` first on PATH and exports `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`. GNU Make 3.81 starts a recipe line without shell syntax itself and finds its program with the PATH that make started with: with the exported PATH, the recipe line `npm --version` printed 11.19.0, the npm of the machine, and `go env GOVERSION` printed go1.27.0, also with `SHELL := /bin/bash`, while GNU Make 4.4.1 printed 12.2.0 and go1.27.1. The recipes therefore start npm as `$(NPM)`, the path of its wrapper, and go and gofmt only in lines with shell syntax. `config/toolchain.json` pins PHP to the minor versions 8.2 and 8.5 and Composer to 2.10.3: setup-php cannot pin a patch; `var/full-run.json` records the Node.js, npm, Go, cargo, PHP and Composer versions of each run and rerun as `environment`. The workflows pin every action by its commit with its release in a comment, run on `ubuntu-24.04`, set `composer:2.10.3`, install with `make install` and take the Go that bootstraps `make install-tools` from setup-go; `make release-check` installs with `make install`. make is not pinned (T17.1-4). `tests/scripts/toolchain-files.test.mjs`, now in `always` of `scripts/owner-checks.json`, checks Node.js, npm, Go, Rust, PHP and Composer of the recipes with the expected and the actual version; on the tree of T19.1, 7 of its 10 cases failed, with `package.json packageManager: expected npm@<major>.<minor>.<patch>, actual undefined`, `go of the recipes is not the one of var/tools/bin`, `tools composer:v2, expected composer:2.10.3`, the dry run of `make install`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml installs without make install` and `actions/checkout@v6 is not pinned by a commit`; its 10 cases pass after the change. The 3 cases of the new `tests/scripts/install-tools.test.mjs` and the new case of `tests/scripts/full-run.test.mjs` pass, `make install-check` passes with Go 1.27.1 of `var/tools`, and the npm of the machine kept its version through the installs.
- Made `tests/scripts/long-steps.test.mjs` remove its temporary directory only after the processes that it started have exited (T20.3). Its case of a long package install step stops the check after the `npm pack` step with SIGKILL to the process group and then removes the directory that holds the check's temporary files; it failed in 1 of 4 runs of `make test-scripts` with `ENOTEMPTY`, because SIGKILL ends the processes of a group soon, not at once, and a process that still ran wrote into the directory while it was removed. With three processes of a group that create directories as fast as they can, killing the group and removing the directory at once failed in 4 of 20 tries. The new helper `tests/scripts/process-group.mjs` sends the signal, waits for the leader to exit and polls until no process of the group is left, without a time limit and saying so on stderr when it waits longer than a second; the test uses it. `tests/scripts/process-group.test.mjs` removes the directory after the helper in 20 of 20 tries; with a helper that does not wait it failed with `ENOTEMPTY` in each of 2 runs.
- Started the browser test's server with an explicit command and made its test independent of PATH and of other builds (T20.2). `tests/browser/run.mjs` looked the server up as `node` on PATH, and `tests/scripts/browser-server.test.mjs` put a slow `node` first on PATH without checking that it ran; one run of `make test-scripts` started the server in 0.6 s, below the 1.5 s of the stub. The test also asserted that `packages/template-ts/dist/index.d.ts` kept its inode, and the prerequisite build that T18.8-2 added to `tests/scripts/build-package.test.mjs` republishes that `dist` when its inputs changed, so the assertion failed when both ran together; with a forced build of template-ts started beside it, the old test failed in 3 of 3 runs. `run.mjs` now starts `<command> tests/browser/server.mjs`, with the Node.js of its own process unless `--server-command` names another command; the test passes a wrapper that records its arguments, waits 1.5 s and runs the server, asserts that the wrapper ran with `tests/browser/server.mjs`, and asserts that the output of run.mjs holds no build step. The new test failed against the old run.mjs and passes alone and in 3 of 3 runs beside a forced build; `make test-browser` passes with the default command. Wave 20 records this task with T20.1 and T20.3.
- Made the package install check read no source of the network and use the toolchains of the checkout (T19.1). The Go install project of `scripts/check-package-installs.mjs` resolved modules from `file://<proxy>,https://proxy.golang.org`, verified checksums against `sum.golang.org` and ran with `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto`: with Go 1.27.0 on the machine and `go 1.27.1` in the install project `go.mod`, every run downloaded the Go toolchain 1.27.1 into the module cache of its workspace. The Rust install project had no `Cargo.lock`, so cargo resolved `serde` and `serde_json` to the newest releases at the time of the run, and it built in the system temporary directory, where `rust-toolchain.toml` does not apply: `cargo --version` there printed 1.98.0, while the checkout pins 1.98.1. The PHP install project kept Packagist as a repository. The new test `tests/scripts/package-installs.test.mjs` runs the check with stub commands that record their arguments, environment and working directory; before the change it failed with `go env GOVERSION has GOPROXY null, expected only the file proxy of the run`. The Go install project now uses only the proxy of the run, `GOSUMDB=off`, `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` and the go directive of `packages/template-go/go.mod`; the Rust install project copies `rust-toolchain.toml` and runs `cargo run --locked` with a lock that `installCargoLock` derives from `packages/template-rust/Cargo.lock`, keeping the locked versions and checksums of the packages that the install project reaches and writing each dependency as cargo writes it; the PHP install project sets `packagist.org` to false. The 2 cases pass, and `make install-check` passed with the Go toolchain 1.27.1 first on PATH, `cargo run --locked` accepting the derived lock.
- Installed the Rust toolchain in one explicit step and let no cargo install it (T18.7-1). CI failed only in `test-scripts`, in one case: the release job had no installed toolchain 1.98.1 of `rust-toolchain.toml`, `test-scripts`, the third target of the full suite, was the first to start cargo, and three of its test files started cargo at once; rustup auto-installed the toolchain in each, and the Rust runner of `tests/scripts/generated-conformance.test.mjs` failed with `error: the 'cargo' binary, normally provided by the 'cargo' component, is not applicable to the '1.98.1-x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu' toolchain`. Locally the toolchain was installed. Three `cargo --version` started together in a fresh `RUSTUP_HOME` failed in 5 of 5 tries, 2 or 3 of the 3 each time, with that message and with conflicts of rustup's own downloads and component files. `make install` now runs `rustup toolchain install --no-self-update` after `npm ci`; the Makefile exports `RUSTUP_AUTO_INSTALL=0` and the CI workflows set it, so a missing toolchain fails at once with `error: toolchain '1.98.1-...' is not installed` and `help: run rustup toolchain install`; the release job runs `make install`, and the rust and php jobs run `rustup toolchain install --no-self-update` instead of `rustup show`, whose auto-install rustup deprecates. In the fresh `RUSTUP_HOME`, cargo without the install failed twice with that message, and after `rustup toolchain install` three `cargo --version` started together passed. The summary of `scripts/run-tests.mjs` said `0 passed, 0 failed, ..., the tool exited with 1` without the cause; when a tool exits without a failing test it now names the last error lines of the tool (`error:`, `error[E...]:`, `fatal error:`, `panic:`, `PHP Fatal error:`) or says that it printed none. The PHPUnit timeout case of `tests/scripts/run-tests.test.mjs`, which failed on a checkout without `packages/template-php/vendor`, runs `composer install` first. The new cases of `tests/scripts/toolchain-files.test.mjs` and `tests/scripts/run-tests.test.mjs` failed before the change and pass after it, also with GNU Make 4.4.1.
- Made the make dry runs of the script tests print only the commands on every make (T17.1-4). The first CI run of the full suite failed only in `test-scripts`, in 2 of its 103 cases: `make -n check` and `make -n release-test-matrix`, started by tests inside the make of `make check`, printed `make[2]: Entering directory` and `Leaving directory` around the guard command, because GNU Make 4 turns on `-w` in a make started by another make; macOS make 3.81, which ran `make owner-check` locally, does not. The dry runs of `tests/scripts/full-run.test.mjs`, `tests/scripts/suite-parity.test.mjs` and `tests/scripts/test-targets.test.mjs` pass `--no-print-directory` and run with `MAKEFLAGS=w`, which makes every make print the lines, so a dry run without the option fails on every make. Before the change the first two failed with `MAKEFLAGS=w` and with GNU Make 4.4.1 at `MAKELEVEL=1`; after it the three pass plain, with `MAKEFLAGS=w` and with GNU Make 4.4.1 at `MAKELEVEL=1`. The make version is not a further toolchain requirement: the Makefile uses no construct newer than GNU Make 3.81, and `make --no-print-directory -n -B <target>` printed the same commands with make 3.81 and GNU Make 4.4.1 for all 82 targets except the path of `$(MAKE)`; scripts/full-run.mjs judges a target by its exit status and scripts/owner-check.mjs passes `--no-print-directory`.
- Made `CHECK_TARGETS` the one list of the full suite and ran it everywhere through the guard (T17.1-2). `make release-test-matrix` ran seven layers of make invocations and stopped at its first failure: CI reported only `dependency-policy-check` and skipped the six other layers, and the remaining phpunit finding in `packages/template-php-ext` appeared only after the fix. The lists also differed: `make check` did not run `dependency-audit`, `benchmark-check`, `benchmark-smoke` and `docs-verify-idempotent`, and no CI job ran `test-scripts`, `language-test-matrix`, `function-contract-check`, `delimiter-matrix` and `generated-native-check`, while `docs-static-check` ran only in the pages job, which a failure skips. `CHECK_TARGETS` now holds the four targets, `make release-test-matrix` runs the guard with `CHECK_TARGETS` like `make check`, and so does `make release-check` in its clean checkout. The release job of the CI workflow runs `xvfb-run -a make check` and uploads `var/full-run.json` also after a failure; every step of a verifying job runs after a failing step (`if: ${{ !cancelled() }}`), and the make of the editor job, which names several targets, runs with `-k`; only the pages job, which publishes, stops at its first failure. `scripts/owner-check.mjs` treats a target whose recipe starts `scripts/full-run.mjs` or `scripts/check-clean-release.mjs` as a full-suite target: the owner of `scripts/check-clean-release.mjs` was `make release-check`, which runs the full suite, and is now `tests/scripts/clean-release.test.mjs`, which runs the script with stub `npm`, `node` and `make` in a temporary repository. `tests/scripts/suite-parity.test.mjs` failed in its 4 cases before the change and passes after it; the new case of `tests/scripts/owner-check.test.mjs` failed before the change and passes after it.
- Made `tests/scripts/build-package.test.mjs` create what it depends on and installed the copy of a package atomically (T18.8-2). On a checkout after `npm ci` with nothing built, 7 of its 12 cases failed with `TS2307: Cannot find module '@polyspec/template'` and `Could not resolve "@polyspec/template-lsp"`: the installed copies of the packages of this repository had no `dist`, and the test relied on `make test-scripts` having built them, which `make owner-check` and a direct run of the test do not do. The test now builds and installs template-ts, template-language and template-lsp with `scripts/build-package.mjs --install` before its cases; with unchanged inputs that does nothing. `--install` reinstalled a copy with `rm -rf` and `npm install`, and `node --test` runs test files concurrently, so a reader of the copy could find it half removed: a new case that polls the copy during a reinstall found 20 files missing in 1377 polls. The install now writes each changed file into `node_modules/<name>.next-<pid>` and moves it into the copy with a rename, the modules that a file imports first and `package.json` last, then removes the files that the package no longer has; it fails with `npm install` as the fix when the dependencies of the package differ from the copy, which is a change of `package-lock.json`. On a fresh `npm ci` checkout the 13 cases pass, with no file missing during the reinstall.
- Checked the dependency state against the review recorded in the repository, without a network (T18.10). `make dependency-policy-check` queried the registries on every run, so the same tree passed one day and failed the next: tree T18.8 failed in CI after phpunit 11.5.57 was released, without a change of the tree, and the check stopped at its first finding, which hid the same version in `packages/template-php-ext`. `scripts/check-dependency-policy.mjs` now reads only the manifests, the locks, `config/dependency-policy.json` and the review record `config/dependency-review.json`, which holds the time of the review, the sha256 and the advisories of every lock and the locked and latest stable version of every registry dependency; it reports every finding in one run, each with its rule and fix, and runs `composer validate` with `COMPOSER_DISABLE_NETWORK=1`. The developer command `make dependency-review` (`scripts/dependency-review.mjs`) asks the registries for newer stable releases and advisories and prints each with its fix; `RECORD=1` writes the record, and `UPDATE=1` raises the dependencies and updates the packages with an advisory first. `make dependency-audit` runs no live `npm audit` or `composer audit`: the gate rejects a lock with an advisory at its review and a lock changed without a review, and the scheduled workflow `.github/workflows/dependency-review.yml` runs the review every day. `file:` packages and platform requirements are outside the review by its scope, and the gate checks the lock of a `file:` package against its version. `scripts/owner-checks.json` selects the dependency gate for the Composer manifests and locks. `tests/scripts/dependency-policy.test.mjs` runs the gate and the review against stub registries: before the change the gate gave two results on one tree with two registries and queried them; its 6 cases pass after it, with no registry call by the gate. The review of 2026-10-05 recorded 27 registry dependencies and 3 locks with no advisory.
- Updated phpunit/phpunit to 11.5.57, the latest stable release, in `packages/template-php` and `packages/template-php-ext` (T18.9). CI failed in `make release-test-matrix`, because `make dependency-policy-check` failed with `outdated Composer dependency has no exception: packages/template-php/composer.json phpunit/phpunit 11.5.56 < 11.5.57`; the check stopped there, and `composer outdated --direct --locked` reported the same version in `packages/template-php-ext`. phpunit 11.5.57 was released on 2026-10-05 and changes only the SBOM of its PHAR. `composer update phpunit/phpunit --with-dependencies` also updated nikic/php-parser to 5.9.0 in both locks, which keep the PHP 8.2.0 platform. After the update `make dependency-audit` passes, `make test-php` passes its 655 tests and `make test-ext` passes 248 of 248 conformance cases and its 313 tests.
- Built template-language, template-lsp, template-codemirror and the VS Code bundle with the mechanism of T18.8 (T18.8-1). One `make check` built them again with the same inputs (tsup 7, 3 and 2 times, esbuild 2 times), each time emptying or rewriting the `dist` that the next target read; no repository outside this one reads them. `scripts/build-ts.mjs` became `scripts/build-package.mjs --package <package>`: it skips a build of unchanged inputs, which now include the root `package-lock.json` and the installed packages of this repository that a package depends on, records the files of the build in `dist.inputs.json`, and moves each file after the modules it imports. The `make build-*` targets of the five packages run it. `tests/scripts/build-package.test.mjs` failed before the change and its 12 cases pass after it.
- Made `make owner-check` select nothing for a removed path that no rule owns and skip a test file that was removed (T13.1-4-1). A rename failed the owner check with the removed name and selected the removed test. The two new cases of `tests/scripts/owner-check.test.mjs` failed before the change and pass after it.
- Selected the owner checks of a change from its paths (T13.1-4). `make owner-check` runs the make targets and node tests that `scripts/owner-checks.json` declares for the changed paths and never the full suite; it fails, naming each, on a tracked path without an owner, a glob without a path, a target that the Makefile lacks and a full-suite target. A change under `tests/cases` runs `make conformance-cases CASES=...`, which runs only those cases in every mode. Every tracked path has an owner: `tests/runner/function-inventory.mjs`, which no target ran, runs in `function-inventory-check` of `make check`, and `.node-version`, `rust-toolchain.toml`, `.gitignore` and `.editorconfig` have tests of what they declare. `.editorconfig` declares the byte-exact test files and the generated showcase files, and the Go examples of six documents are indented with spaces. AGENTS refers to `make owner-check` instead of a rule to remember. The new tests failed before the change and pass after it.
- Published the build of the TypeScript package without a moment of missing files and built it only when its inputs change (T18.8). While `make check` rebuilt `packages/template-ts/dist` with tsup, whose `clean: true` empties `dist` first, a reader of `dist` found its declarations missing until the declaration build ended; one `make check` ran that build 17 times with the same inputs. `make build-ts` now runs `scripts/build-ts.mjs --install`: it skips the build when the hash of its inputs is unchanged, builds into a directory next to `dist`, moves each file into `dist` with a rename, the imported modules first and the entries last, removes the files of the previous build afterwards, and installs the npm copy again only when it differs. `docs/operations/development.md` states the remaining limit for a reader that reads several files during a build of changed sources. A reader that polled `dist` during a tsup build found the declarations missing in 413 of 1559 polls; `tests/scripts/build-ts.test.mjs` failed before the change and passes after it, with no missing file.
- Built the Rust crates of each checkout into their own target (T18.7). `make check` failed in `test-rust`: a worktree had built the tests into the target of the main checkout through `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")` embedded the path of that worktree, which was removed later, and cargo took the 5 test binaries as fresh because the sources of the main checkout were older, so 6 of the 7 tests of `tests/bound.rs` failed with `repository root: NotFound`. The Makefile no longer passes an inherited `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` to cargo, the tests resolve the repository root from `CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR` at run time, AGENTS states the rule, and `cargo clean -p polyspec-template` removed the stale artifacts from the target of the main checkout. `tests/scripts/cargo-target.test.mjs` failed before the change and passes after it; `make test-rust` compiled the crate and passed its 49 tests.
- Linked the Korean execution plan to the checklist by its site route `/ko/plans/execution-checklist` (T13.1-3). `make check` failed in `docs-static-check`, because the relative link `execution-checklist.ko.md` became the source-only route `./execution-checklist.ko`; the commit that added it ran `make docs-check` and `make docs`, which do not check the links of the built site. AGENTS now requires `make docs-static-check` with `make docs-check` before a commit that changes a file under `docs/`, and T13.1-4 records selecting owner checks from changed paths. `make docs-static-check` failed before the change and passes after it.
- Failed `scripts/check-documents.mjs` on the translation link line of the checklist, which now holds only headings and task tables, and removed that line from both languages (T13.1-2). T13.1-1 accepted a link on the third line of the checklist, although AGENTS states that every document has a `.ko.md` file. The new case of `tests/scripts/check-documents.test.mjs` failed before the change, because a checklist with that line passed; its 6 cases pass after it.
- Made the checklist hold only tasks and its markers only as task states (T13.1-1). The legend and four texts of `docs/plans/execution-checklist.md` wrote state markers and task list markers, and a tool that counted markers reported tasks in progress that did not exist; 93 lines of each language were not tasks. `scripts/check-documents.mjs` now fails, with the file, line and column and with no exception, on a line of the checklist other than its title, the translation link, a heading or a task table row, and on a state marker or `[x]`/`[X]` anywhere other than the start of the last cell of a task row; it reads derived sub-items such as `T14.2-1` as task rows, whose states it did not validate before. The introduction, dependency overview, wave dependencies, causes and exit criteria, parallelism summary and definition of done with its evidence moved to `docs/plans/execution-plan.md`; the task ID format and the content of a task row moved to AGENTS, which defines the states; the task texts write states in words. `tests/scripts/check-documents.test.mjs` failed before the change in 3 of its 5 cases, because the fixtures with stray markers, a paragraph and a derived sub-item with the state `done` passed the checker; its 5 cases pass after it.
- Added the guard of the full suite `scripts/full-run.mjs` (T17.1-1). AGENTS stated that `make check` runs once, when every active task is done, and nothing enforced it: `make check` started its first step with a task in progress, with uncommitted changes and on a tree it had already verified. `make check` now starts the guard before any step. The guard prints its decision with the reason and refuses while a task of `docs/plans/execution-checklist.md` is `[~]`, listing each active ID with its task, while tracked changes are uncommitted, and when `var/full-run.json` records a full run of the current tree, naming that run. It runs each target of `CHECK_TARGETS` with `make <target>` to its end and writes the record before and after each target, so a stopped run stays `incomplete`. `make rerun-failed` reruns only the targets of the current tree that did not pass and completes the result when they pass. The CI workflow runs targets in separate jobs and not `make check`. `tests/scripts/full-run.test.mjs` failed with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` before the change, while `make -n check` printed the first step of `docs-check`; its 10 cases pass with stub targets after it.
- Updated eslint to 10.12.0, the latest stable release (T18.6). `make dependency-policy-check`, part of `make release-check`, failed with `outdated npm dependency has no exception: eslint 10.11.0 < 10.12.0` and passes after the update; `make lint-js` passes. The manifests of the five generated JavaScript showcase artifacts and `examples/site/index.html` record the digest of the changed `package-lock.json` (`make typed-generator`); their outputs are unchanged.
- Made `make install-cli` install `template-fmt` without a symbolic link (T18.5). `npm link` linked the package and its command into the global npm directories, and the `npm install --global --install-links` of T18.4 failed with `404` because npm resolved `@polyspec/template` 0.0.1 from the registry. `scripts/install-cli.mjs` writes the npm project `<prefix>/lib/polyspec-template-fmt`, which installs copies of the formatter and template packages without bin links, and the executable script `<prefix>/bin/template-fmt`, which runs node with the entry of the copied formatter by its absolute path; the prefix is `CLI_PREFIX` (default `~/.local`), and `make uninstall-cli` removes both. `make test-scripts` builds the formatter first. `tests/scripts/install-cli.test.mjs` installs into a temporary prefix, finds no symbolic link there, runs `template-fmt --check` on the formatter fixtures and uninstalls; it failed without the script and passes with it.
- Installed every npm dependency as a copy without bin links (T18.4). npm linked the five workspace packages into `node_modules` and wrote 37 bin links into `node_modules/.bin`: 42 symbolic links in the main checkout. `.npmrc` sets `install-links=true` and `bin-links=false`. The root `package.json` declares `@polyspec/template`, `@polyspec/template-language`, `@polyspec/template-lsp`, `@polyspec/template-codemirror` and `polyspec-template` as `file:` dependencies with overrides for the dependencies between them, instead of npm workspaces, which npm always links, and holds the development tools that the packages declared; the packages keep their runtime dependencies and have no npm scripts. The esbuild of the VS Code extension and the CodeMirror browser test is the pinned root esbuild 0.27.2. Each build target installs the npm copy of its package again after its build, `make install` runs `npm ci`, the new `make lint-js` runs ESLint, and the recipes, the runners and the checks start TypeScript, tsup, esbuild, Vitest, Playwright, ESLint, VitePress, vsce and vscode-tmgrammar-test with the file of their package (`scripts/tools.mjs`); CI uses the same commands. The dependency policy reads only the root manifest and the exception for `@types/vscode` names it. `make install-cli` installs a copy of the formatter package globally instead of linking it. `tests/scripts/no-symlinks.test.mjs` listed the 42 links of the main checkout and passes on a checkout installed with the change. The manifests of the five generated JavaScript showcase artifacts record the digest of the changed `package-lock.json` (`make typed-generator`), and `examples/site/index.html`, which shows them, is built again; their outputs are unchanged.
- Made `tests/scripts/browser-server.test.mjs` run `tests/browser/run.mjs` against the package build that `make test-scripts` makes first (T18.3). It ran `make test-browser`, whose package build empties `packages/template-ts/dist` (tsup `clean: true`) and writes it again while the other tests of `make test-scripts` run, so `make check` failed in `tests/scripts/generated-conformance.test.mjs` with `TS7016: Could not find a declaration file for module '@polyspec/template'`. The test now also requires that `dist/index.d.ts` keeps its inode and modification time: with `make test-browser` it failed on that assertion, and with `tests/browser/run.mjs` it passes.
- Gave the VS Code directory `.vscode-test` one holder at a time (T18.2). Every run in one checkout downloads and unpacks VS Code into that directory, and `make clean` removed it while an integration run used it. The new `scripts/holder-lock.mjs` creates a lock file atomically with its whole content (a complete file under its own name, then linked to the lock path, which fails when the lock exists) and names the checkout, the process ID and the start time of the holder; only the holder releases it, also when it exits through a signal. `tests/integration/run.mjs` requires the option `--cache` and holds `<cache>.lock` from the download to its last launch; `make clean` removes `.vscode-test` through `make clean-vscode-test`, which holds the lock while it removes the directory. A second run and `make clean` fail with the holder. A lock whose process has ended is reported with that holder and stays until `make vscode-test-unlock` removes it, which fails while the holder runs. `tests/scripts/holder-lock.test.mjs` failed in all five cases before the change and passes after it.
- Made the static server of `make test-browser` listen on a port that the system assigns (T18.1). It listened on the fixed port `127.0.0.1:4173`, so a concurrent run of any checkout failed with `EADDRINUSE` while the first run held the port, and Playwright loaded its page from that port whichever server held it. `tests/browser/server.mjs` now prints the assigned port in its line `listening on http://127.0.0.1:<port>`, `tests/browser/run.mjs` passes that address to Playwright in `TEMPLATE_BROWSER_URL`, and `playwright.config.ts` fails without it; the root script `test:browser` runs `tests/browser/run.mjs`. `tests/scripts/browser-port.test.mjs` holds `127.0.0.1:4173` with another server and runs the browser test: the static server exited with `EADDRINUSE` before the change, and the browser case passes on the assigned port after it.
- Started the static server of `make test-browser` as a step without a time limit (T17.11). Playwright started it through `webServer` with a startup deadline of 30 s and printed none of its output; Playwright reads `timeout: 0` as 60 s, so the deadline cannot be removed there. `tests/browser/run.mjs` now starts `tests/browser/server.mjs`, prints its output with the prefix `[server]`, waits until the server prints `listening on http://127.0.0.1:4173`, fails when the server exits first, and then runs Playwright; each case keeps its timeout of 60 s. `tests/scripts/browser-server.test.mjs` runs `make test-browser` with every `node` started by name delayed by 1.5 s: with the startup deadline set to 0.5 s it failed with `Timed out waiting 500ms from config.webServer`, and now the server starts and the browser case passes.
- Ran the vitest tests of `test-ts`, `test-language`, `test-lsp` and `test-codemirror` and the `node --test` tests of `test-vscode` through `scripts/run-tests.mjs` (T17.10). They ran through `npm test` and `npm run test:extension`, which printed a test only after its file had ended and gave the `node --test` tests no timeout. Each test now prints its start and its result with its elapsed time and has its own timeout of 30 s; the package script `test:extension` is removed. `tests/scripts/test-targets.test.mjs` reads the commands of the five targets with `make -n`: it failed for all five before the change and passes after it, and the five targets pass with 790 tests in `test-ts`.
- Made `tests/runner/delimiter-matrix.mjs` print `<pair> [<lang>] pass|fail (<ms> ms)` when a delimiter pair finishes in a language (T17.9). The matrix runs 3624 CLI calls in about 150 s and printed one line after the last of them. The time is spent in the CLI calls: a call takes about 44 ms in TypeScript and 59 ms in PHP, and the builds run before the matrix in the Makefile prerequisites. `tests/scripts/delimiter-matrix-progress.test.mjs` printed nothing within 10 s before the change and reads the first three result lines after it.
- Ran each command of `scripts/check-package-installs.mjs` and `scripts/check-install-workspace.mjs` as a logged step without a time limit (T17.8). The commands pack, install, build and run the install projects, and ran under limits of 600 s and 120 s without printing anything; a limit fails a normal run that is slower than expected. Each command now runs through `runStepSync`, prints its start, its output and its result with the elapsed time on standard error and fails when it exits with a nonzero status. `tests/scripts/long-steps.test.mjs` delays `zip` and `npm` by 1.5 s: with the limits set to 0.5 s the checks failed with `spawnSync zip ETIMEDOUT` and `npm pack ... failed`, and without the limits the steps pass with their result lines.
- Ran the build of a driver in `tests/runner/drivers.mjs` as a logged step without the limit of 600 s (T17.7). A build is a long-running operation; it printed nothing and was killed at the limit. The new `runStepSync` of `scripts/test-progress/step.mjs` prints the start, the output of the build and the result with the elapsed time on standard error and fails when the build exits with a nonzero status; each CLI call keeps its timeout of 10 s. `tests/scripts/long-steps.test.mjs` builds with a command of 1.5 s: with the limit set to 0.5 s the build failed with `slow: build failed`, and without the limit it passes with its result line.
- Removed the deadlines of the profile installation (120 s) and of the VS Code launch (300 s) of the VS Code integration test (T17.5-2). An installation and a launch are long-running operations, and a deadline fails a normal run that is slower than expected. The download, each installation and each launch now run as steps of `packages/template-vscode/tests/integration/step.mjs` without a time limit: each prints its start, a line every 10 s and its result with its elapsed time, the installation also prints the output of VS Code, and each step is judged by its exit code or by the result of its suite. Each check keeps its timeout of 20 s. The 20 s exit grace of T17.5-1 starts after the suite result has been printed; it ends VS Code as cleanup and does not decide the result. `tests/integration-step.test.mjs` runs a step of 1.5 s: with the deadline set to 0.5 s it failed with `slow step exceeded its 0.5 s deadline`, and without the deadline it ends by its exit code.
- Removed the 300 s deadline of the `tsc` step of `tests/runner/conformance-generated-ts.mjs` (T17.4-1). `tsc` is a long-running operation, and a deadline fails a normal run that is slower than expected. The step now runs through `runStep` of `tests/runner/bounded.mjs` without a time limit, still prints its start, a line every 5 s and its result with the elapsed time, and fails when `tsc` exits with a nonzero code; each render keeps its deadline of 30 s. A new test in `tests/scripts/generated-conformance.test.mjs` delays `npx` by 1.5 s: with the deadline set to 0.5 s the runner failed with `tsc exceeded its 0.5 s deadline`, and without the deadline it passes with the `tsc` result line.
- Stated in AGENTS that a long-running operation, such as a build, a `tsc` type check, an installation, a download, the installation or launch of a program or a whole run, prints a log line for each step and has no timeout, also no deadline for missing output, and is judged by its exit status, its result and its errors; a test case keeps its own timeout (T17.6). A time limit on a long operation fails a normal run that is slower than expected. `AGENTS.md` now also states that English documents are canonical, which only `AGENTS.ko.md` stated.
- Made the Rust part of `scripts/check-typed-generator.mjs` read the `RequestError` that `Program::render` returns since T15.2 (T15.2-1). The test it generates declared `Result<String, TemplateError>` and failed to compile with E0308, so `typed-generator-compile-check` and `make check` failed. The template error of the output limit is now read from `RequestError::Template`; the check passes.
- Ended a VS Code launch of the integration test by the result of its suite (T17.5-1). VS Code 1.138 took 4 s to more than 300 s to exit after the suite of the launch without capabilities had printed its result, with nothing printed meanwhile, so `make check` failed at the launch deadline of 300 s. Once the line `[suite] N of M checks passed` appears, VS Code now has 20 s to exit; then its process group is killed and the launch takes the suite result. Two runs ended the launches after 22.6 s and 25.2 s through the grace, and a failing check still failed its launch.
- Gave the VS Code integration test a deadline for each step and a timeout for each check (T17.5). `packages/template-vscode/tests/integration/run.mjs` waited for each profile installation and each VS Code launch without a limit, and `suite/index.cjs` waited for each check without a limit: with a check that did not end, the run printed nothing for 136 s after the suite started, until it was stopped from outside. A profile installation now has a deadline of 120 s and a launch a deadline of 300 s, after which the process is killed with its process group and the step fails by its name with its elapsed time. Each check has a timeout of 20 s through `Promise.race`, prints its start, and prints its elapsed time on its `ok` and `not ok` line; with the same check, the run now fails with `not ok - <check> (20002 ms)`.
- Gave every case of the four generated conformance runners its own deadline and a result line with its elapsed time (T17.4). The Go and Rust runners ran all cases in one `go test` or `cargo test` call under one limit of 600 s, the PHP runner ran each case without a limit, the TypeScript runner rendered each case in its own process without a limit, and all four printed only a final count: with a case whose data parsing did not end, each runner printed nothing for 90 s. The Go and Rust runners now run their case tests through `scripts/run-tests.mjs` (30 s for each test), and the Rust tests are named after their cases (`case_echo_path`) instead of their index. The PHP runner runs `php -l` and the case process under a deadline of 30 s each, and the TypeScript runner runs `tsc` under a deadline of 300 s with a step line and renders each case in a worker under a deadline of 30 s. With the same case, each runner now fails by the case name with its elapsed time. `tests/scripts/generated-conformance.test.mjs` checks the result lines of each runner and the deadlines of `tests/runner/bounded.mjs`.
- Made `tests/runner/conformance.mjs` print `<case> [<lang>] pass|fail (<ms> ms)` when a case finishes in an implementation (T17.3, CNF-10). It printed nothing until the last case had run in every implementation and then printed the summary table, which it still prints. `tests/scripts/conformance-progress.test.mjs` failed before the change because the table header was the first line, and passes now. `make test-scripts` builds the TypeScript package first.
- Added the test runner `scripts/run-tests.mjs` with `scripts/test-progress/` (T17.2). It runs node, vitest, go, cargo and phpunit, prints each test when it starts, while it runs and when it ends, with its elapsed time, and stops the tool when a test outlives its own timeout (30 s). `test-go`, `test-rust` and `test-php` run through it. `test-go` limited each package to 120 s and printed nothing until a package ended: a hanging Go test printed nothing for 123 s and then a panic. A hanging Rust test printed only the libtest notice after 60 s and a hanging PHPUnit test printed nothing; neither ended. Through the runner each of them fails by its name with its elapsed time. PHPUnit also stops a test after 10 s with `enforceTimeLimit` and fails the run on it with `failOnRisky`, and the runner keeps every failure message of a test. `make test-scripts`, part of `make check`, runs the tests of the runner in `tests/scripts/run-tests.test.mjs`.
- Stated the test run rules in AGENTS (T17.1): a task in progress runs only the Red and Green tests that own the change, a commit also runs `make docs-check`, a task becomes `[o]` after its owning command passes, and `make check` runs once when every active task is done. AGENTS had required `make check` before any task was marked done. Every test prints its progress and has its own timeout, no whole run has a time limit, and the Verification column of a task names its owning command. Added the section "Decision and acceptance rules" to `AGENTS.ko.md`, which lacked it.
- Stated that renders of one program may run at the same time after the host registered its functions and classes (T16.1, RT-70), and made the template cache of the Go program safe for it with a mutex. Two goroutines that rendered one Go AST program raced on the cache map; `TestConcurrentRendersShareOneProgram` failed under `go test -race` before the change and passes now, and the bound map test shares one program. A Rust program is neither `Send` nor `Sync`, so a Rust host creates one program per thread.
- Made Go `Parse` return the argument error of Go, an `error` that is not an `*errs.Error`, for a delimiter option that is not a delimiter pair, as `NewAstProgram` already did (T15.3-1, ERR-13). It returned E_DATA_UNSUPPORTED_TYPE, an ERR-1 error. `TestInvalidDelimiterIsArgumentError` failed on `Parse` before the change and passes now.
- Made the Go typed generated program read an absent optional field as null, as the TypeScript, Rust and PHP generated programs do (T15.4). The Go backend read an optional field through `valueOrZero`, so `{= note ?? '-'}` with an absent `string?` field rendered an empty string; the field now stays a pointer, a nil pointer is null, and `??` and the ternary convert an operand to the pointer or value type of their result. Verified with `node scripts/check-generated-typed-values.mjs`, which failed on Go with the new `note ?? '-'` case and now passes in TypeScript, Go, Rust and PHP; the committed generated programs and the example site are regenerated (`make typed-generator-check` passes).
- The TypeScript package writes the CommonJS file of each entry into the output directory of its build (T14.2-2). It wrote `dist/<entry>.cjs` relative to the working directory, so a build with `--out-dir` failed with `ENOENT`.
- The Rust engine names the bound inputs of a request as `BoundRequest` (T14.2-1). The tuple that T14.2 returned failed `cargo clippy -D warnings` with `type_complexity`.
- Made Rust and the PHP extension report a delimiter option that is not a delimiter pair as an argument error (T15.3, ERR-13). Rust `AstProgram::new` panicked and now returns `Result<AstProgram, ArgumentError>`; Rust `parse` returned an ERR-1 `E_PARSE_INVALID_DIRECTIVE` and now returns `RequestError::Argument`; the PHP extension raised an `\Exception` and now raises an `\InvalidArgumentException`, as the PHP package does. The command line program ends the error with status 1 and no longer checks the option itself. `tests/delimiters.rs` did not compile against the old signatures and passes now; the two extension tests in `EngineTest.php` failed with `\Exception` before the change and pass after it.
- Added bound data (T14.2, VAL-22, ERR-14, RT-61): `bind` checks host data once and returns a bound map, `merge` combines two bound maps by the precedence of RT-26, and `render` and `prepare` take a bound map as `assign` and as definition `data` without binding it again, in TypeScript and its browser entry, Go, Rust, PHP, the PHP extension and the four generated programs. A bound map at any other position, a bound map of another implementation and a non-nil Go pointer to one fail with E_DATA_UNSUPPORTED_TYPE; errors of `bind` and `merge` have an empty template and no position. Renamed the host binding functions that had the name of an operation: TypeScript `bind` to `bindValue`, Go `value.Bind` to `value.BindValue` and Rust `bind` to `bind_json`; Rust `DefineInput.data` is a `DefineData`, and Rust programs take a bound map through `prepare_bound` and `render_bound`. The TypeScript build emits ES modules only and each CommonJS entry loads its ES module, because the separate CommonJS bundles each held a copy of the runtime, so a bound map of `index.cjs` failed in `render.cjs`. `tools/compiler/interface.json` names the type, its operations and the operations that take it in every implementation, including the new `php-extension` and `javascript-esm` entries, and the interface checks reject an undeclared operation (17 mutations). The shared fixture `tests/fixtures/bound-data` drives the T14.2 tests of every runtime and `scripts/check-generated-bound-data.mjs`; each failed before the implementation and passes after it, together with the compiler interface check and `scripts/check-rules.mjs`. `make check` has not run. Registered T15.4, an absent optional field that the Go typed generated program reads as the zero value, and T16.1, renders that share one program, which race in the Go template cache.
- Made a generated Rust program report a request whose `assign` or definition `data` does not match the declared types, or whose definitions the program does not declare, as an argument error and not as an ERR-1 error (T15.2, ERR-13). `prepare` and `render` of the Rust `Program` trait, `Engine` and the generated programs return `RequestError`, with `RequestError::Template` for an ERR-1 error and `RequestError::Argument` for an `ArgumentError`; generated Rust reported `E_DATA_UNSUPPORTED_TYPE`. TypeScript, Go and PHP already failed with an `Error`, a Go `error` that is not an `*errs.Error` and an `\InvalidArgumentException`; ERR-13 now names the argument error of each language. `scripts/check-generated-arguments.mjs`, part of `make generated-native-check`, renders `tests/fixtures/typed-arguments` in the four generated programs with a matching request and with a mismatching `assign` and definition `data`; it failed in Rust before the change and passes in all four after it. The command line program ends an argument error with status 1, the Rust showcase adapter reports its message, and `internal_boundary` returns the error type of its operation.
- Made the generated TypeScript and PHP programs bind a null `assign` as an empty map, as their AST programs do (T15.1-1, RT-4); `bindMap` and `Bind::map` received the null and failed. Made `--case group/name | group` select the cases of the four generated conformance runners, which read only a positional filter, so `--case data/null-assign` ran 0 cases and passed; a filter that selects no case now fails. With `--case data/null-assign` the generated TypeScript and PHP runners failed before the change and pass after it; Go and Rust passed before. The support level of `tools/compiler/interface.json`, the compiler and runtime specifications and the testing document count the 248 canonical cases; T15.1 added `data/null-assign` and left them at 247, which failed the compiler interface check.
- Made Rust and the PHP extension render a null `assign` as an empty map, as TypeScript, Go and PHP do (T15.1, RT-4). Rust `bind_map` and the extension `php_to_map` failed with E_DATA_UNSUPPORTED_TYPE. The new case `data/null-assign` failed in Rust and the PHP extension before the change and passes in all five implementations after it. RT-4 states the rule.
- Made PHP host binding check strings, map keys and define ids with `mb_check_encoding` through `Utf8::isValid` (T14.1). `Utf8::firstInvalid`, a PHP loop over every byte, took most of the binding time: binding a map of 1442 Korean strings (81 KB) took p50 4.10 ms before and 0.53 ms after the change (200 runs, opcache, no profiler). `Source` and `Json` keep `firstInvalid` because they report the position of the first invalid byte. `tests/Utf8Test.php` compares both checks on every 1-byte and 2-byte sequence, on every 3-byte and 4-byte sequence of 19 boundary bytes and on named cases (U+10FFFF, surrogates, overlongs, noncharacters, truncated sequences); it passed on PHP 8.5.10 and 8.2.34. Registered T14.2, binding data once for several renders.
- Made the execution checklist use four task states, `[ ]` waiting, `[~]` in progress, `[o]` done and `[!] cause: <cause>; retry: <condition>` bypassed (T13.1). `scripts/check-documents.mjs` accepts no other state and failed on the 129 `[x]` rows of each language before they were written as `[o]`. AGENTS states the checklist rules.
- Fixed the parsing of a ternary whose then-branch ends with an identifier. The TypeScript, Go, Rust and PHP expression lexers produced two `COLON` tokens for `::`, and the parsers read any IDENT followed by `:` as the start of a class call, so `c ? a : b` and `c ? a :b` failed with `E_PARSE_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` (`unexpected token "b"`) in every implementation, the PHP extension and the four generated programs, while `c ? 1 : 2` and `c ? a.x : b` parsed. The lexers now produce the operator `::` as the token `DOUBLE_COLON` by longest match (EXP-1, CNF-13), the grammar lists `class-call` in `postfix` (EXP-7), and a class call starts only at an IDENT followed by `::` (EXP-11); `Order: :f(x)` is now `E_PARSE_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`. The new case `expr/ternary-identifier-branch` covers `c ? a : b`, `c ? a : b.x`, a nested ternary whose then-branch ends with an identifier and `:` without a following space; it failed in all five AST implementations and all four generated programs before the change. The expression fixtures add `c ? a : b`, a class call in a ternary branch and the separated-colon error, and change the tokens of `Order::status_label(x)` to `DOUBLE_COLON`. The formatter of `@polyspec/template-language` writes no space around the `::` token instead of joining two `:` tokens, and its expression fixture adds `c ? a : b`, a nested ternary and a class call in a ternary branch. The canonical case has no class call, because the command line programs cannot register a class function and the generated compiler rejects a class function that the type manifest does not declare.
- Made the generated TypeScript conformance runner skip `tsc` when no selected case compiled. `tsc` without input files printed its help and failed, so the runner reported that exception instead of the compile failures.
- Fixed the dependency policy check, which read every outdated npm dependency as one of the root package.json and skipped a dependency that several workspaces share, because `npm outdated --json` reports it as an array; an outdated `@types/node` passed unnoticed. The check now finds the manifest of the dependent workspace, reads both forms and first proves that it rejects both cases. Updated `@types/node` to 26.6.4 and recorded the exception for `@types/vscode` 1.138.0: vsce refuses an `@types/vscode` newer than `engines.vscode` (^1.138.0), and the extension needs no API of VS Code 1.140. CI had failed on the new `@types/vscode` 1.140.0 release.
- Ran the formatter and editor packages in CI: the job `editor` runs the editor boundary, language service, formatter, language server, CodeMirror and VS Code checks and the VS Code integration test under `xvfb-run`, and the `pages` job waits for it. No CI job had run these packages.
- Fixed the documentation build: the parallelism row of Wave 11 in the execution checklist was a table cell of the form `{…}`, which VitePress read as an attribute list, so `vitepress build` failed. Added `docs-static-check` to `make check`, which had not built the site.
- Updated the README and the documentation index: the generated programs of the typed compiler, editor support with its four packages, the `%` sigil, the current scope of `make check`, the compiler specification, the operations documents that the index and the navigation missed, and the title "Formatter, language server and editors".
- Added `make editor-boundary-check` to `make check`: it fails when an editor adapter declares or imports `@polyspec/template` (EDT-2) or when the language service imports an editor, Node.js or DOM module outside its command line entry (EDT-3), and it first proves that it rejects each kind of violation. Recorded the evidence of EDT-1 to EDT-17, the feature rows `template-lsp` and `template-codemirror` and the VS Code row as a client of the language server.
- Made the VS Code extension a client of the bundled language server `template-lsp` with `vscode-languageclient` (EDT-15). The build bundles the server into `dist/server.cjs`, so the installed extension still needs no repository. Diagnostics, formatting, highlights and folding come from the server, and the in-process formatting, diagnostics and structure providers are removed. The extension keeps the TextMate grammar, paints the tag backgrounds from `polyspec-template/tagRanges` and moves the cursor of Go to Matching Template Tag with `polyspec-template/matchingTag`. It adds semantic tokens, contributes the token types `delimiter` and `path`, and sets `editor.formatOnType` for the language as a configuration default, so the server indents the current line after Enter, `>` and `}`. The extension returns `tagRanges(document)` as its API, which the integration test reads because VS Code cannot read decorations. The integration test in VS Code 1.138.0 checks tag backgrounds, semantic tokens and indentation while typing in addition to the earlier checks.
- Added the Language Server Protocol server `@polyspec/template-lsp` with the command `template-lsp` (EDT-14). It serves published diagnostics, semantic tokens, folding ranges, document highlights, document and range formatting, on-type indentation after `\n`, `>` and `}`, and the requests `polyspec-template/tagRanges` and `polyspec-template/matchingTag` over standard input and output. It analyzes each document version once with `openDocument()`, converts UTF-16 string indexes to protocol positions and reads `polyspec-template.format.templateBlocks` with `workspace/configuration`. `make test-lsp`, part of `make check`, starts the command as a child process and compares its protocol results with every editor fixture (EDT-17) and, for a text with a byte order mark, CRLF line ends and characters outside the Basic Multilingual Plane, with the language service.
- Added the CodeMirror 6 adapter `@polyspec/template-codemirror` (EDT-16). `template(options)` returns an extension with the HTML language of `@codemirror/lang-html`, a state field that analyzes each document text once with `openDocument()`, marks with the classes `cm-template-<type>` on the template tokens, the tag background `#16351c` (dark) and `#e3f6dd` (light) on every tag, lint diagnostics with the error code, folding of closed constructs, matching tag highlights at the main cursor, an indentation service from `lineIndentation` with the editor's indent unit and `templateBlocks`, the command `formatTemplate` on Shift+Alt+F and the command `goToMatchingTag`. Shift+Alt+F is bound to the physical key F, because a keymap entry `Shift-Alt-f` never runs on macOS, where Option+Shift+F types `Ï`. The adapter depends on `@polyspec/template-language` and not on `@polyspec/template`, and declares the CodeMirror packages as peer dependencies. `make test-codemirror`, part of `make check`, compares the adapter's results through an `EditorState` with every editor fixture and runs a Chromium test of typing, token classes, tag backgrounds, diagnostics and Shift+Alt+F.
- Made the package install check remove its temporary directory. Go created the module cache files in that directory read-only, the removal failed, and the check ignored the failure, so every finished run left a directory of 243 MB in the system temporary directory. Go now creates the module cache with `-modcacherw`, a failed removal fails the check with the path of the directory, and an install failure together with a removal failure reports both. `scripts/check-install-workspace.mjs`, run by `make install-check`, downloads a Go module into a workspace and requires that no directory remains, and requires that a removal blocked by a read-only directory fails with the workspace path; both cases failed before the change.
- Indent lines in the formatter by the nesting of HTML elements and template blocks. The `indent` option sets the unit (two spaces by default; `null` keeps the indentation), and `templateBlocks: 'flat'` makes template blocks add no level. Lines inside `<pre>`, `<textarea>`, `<script>`, `<style>`, comments and tags keep their indentation; an HTML structure that is not balanced returns the error `reason: 'html'`. The safety invariant compares ASTs without the spaces and tabs at the start of lines, and the invariant test checks every repository template with and without indentation. `template-fmt` takes `--indent N|tab|keep` and `--template-blocks indent|flat`; the VS Code extension uses the editor indent unit and the setting `polyspec-template.format.templateBlocks`.
- Listed every comment of a template in `Template.comments` (AST-9). A comment produced no node, so a tool could read the text of a template from the parser but not its comments. Each `Comment` node has the source text between the sigil `*` and the terminator as `value` and the span of the tag, including a wrapper; comments inside block bodies are listed in source order. The TypeScript, Go, Rust and PHP parsers and the PHP extension produce the list, the Go and Rust AST decoders require it, the AST schema defines it, and the TypeScript and Rust packages export the `Comment` type. Every expected AST has the field, and the new cases `text/comment-list` and `delimiters/comment-custom` pin values, spans, wrapped and empty comments, a comment in a loop body and custom delimiters.
- Made `runtime-interface-check` and `compiler-interface-check` install the PHP dependencies they run; in a new checkout `make check` failed there because `vendor/bin/phpunit` did not exist. Recorded the definition-of-done evidence of 2026-10-02 in the execution checklist.
- Made template tags stand out in VS Code whatever the color theme is. The grammar gives delimiters and sigils `keyword.control` scopes instead of `punctuation.definition.tag`, which HTML uses for `<` and `>`, and block names `entity.name.type`; the extension paints a background on every tag except comments from the tag ranges of the parser: the deep green `#16351c` in dark themes and `#e3f6dd` in light themes, a lightness step away from the editor background that keeps every syntax color readable. `templateStructure()` returns every tag range for it.
- Moved the conversion of typed generated Go values into the runtime: `value.Convert` returns the template value of a `value.Source`, and generated records and typed maps implement it. Generated Go source no longer imports `reflect`, which `typed-generator-compile-check` forbids; a value test pins a record inside a slice, a nil record and a native object.
- Created the namespaced generated class in the PHP install project; the required PHP namespace had made the class without a namespace unreachable.
- Read the definitions and the environment of browser test cases with the template JSON parser, as the command line programs read them, so that an invalid JSON environment case reports `E_DATA_INVALID_JSON` in the browser.
- Made the Rust and PHP runners of `typed-generator-compile-check` pass definitions in the current host form: the Rust runner reads them with `defines_from_json`, and the PHP runner passes each entry as an array and creates the namespaced generated class.
- Rebuilt `examples/site/index.html` from the current generated sources. The stored benchmark results are unchanged, because a new measurement under a load average of 20 to 30 would not describe the code.
- Added `typed-generator-compile-check`, `install-check`, `test-browser` and `showcase-check` to `make check`; the defects above passed `make check` and failed the release matrix.

- Implemented native assign object binding and logical class function calls in the four AST runtimes. Added the shared runtime contract, Red/Green tests and generated backend call emission.
- Passed the complete 216-case generated conformance matrix in TypeScript, Go, Rust and PHP, and added a four-language generated native object/class-call execution check.
- Completed the native call matrix for output, missing members, missing class functions, argument failures and thrown errors across all four generated backends.

- Resolved Composer lockfiles against the declared PHP 8.2 minimum and added a policy gate that rejects stale or mismatched platform resolution.
- Made the generated-program compile gate install its declared PHP runtime dependency, so the standalone showcase and documentation publication checks run from a clean checkout.
- Published every Korean documentation pair under the `/ko/` locale with Korean navigation and `ko-KR` HTML metadata, and added a static-site regression gate for locale routing and unresolved theme interpolation.
- Updated the Pages configure, artifact upload and deployment actions to their current stable majors.
- Disabled the Go dependency cache in CI because the package has no external modules or checksum file, avoiding an invalid root-module probe.
- Moved static Pages publication behind the complete CI matrix and removed the parallel documentation workflow and its duplicate document and showcase checks.

- Specified the form of host arguments (VAL-21) and made every implementation follow it. A host function, a logical class function and an instance method receive plain values of their language: a safe string is a string, a list is a new list, a map is a new ordered map (TypeScript `Map`, Go `*value.OrderedMap`, Rust `Value::Map`, PHP array in entry order), a number is a PHP `float`, and a native object is the original object. The PHP AST runtime passed `MapValue` and `SafeString` objects while the PHP extension passed arrays and strings; TypeScript, Go and Rust passed safe strings; TypeScript and Go passed the template's own lists and maps, so a host function could change a value that the template read later; Go rejected a `null` argument of a method parameter that accepts nil; the PHP extension converted the map key `-0` to the integer key `0`.
- Specified the equality of native objects (EXP-39): two native objects are equal when they are the same host object, and an ordering comparison of a native object is `E_RUNTIME_COMPARE`. TypeScript compared wrappers, so the same instance bound twice was unequal; Go and Rust never reported equality; the PHP AST runtime failed with `E_INTERNAL`; the PHP extension wrapped each binding of a PHP object anew. `TemplateObject::identity` reports the identity of a Rust native object, and the extension reports the PHP object.
- Added the error codes `E_LOAD_FAILED` (RT-9, RT-10, ERR-6, ERR-9) and `E_DATA_INVALID_JSON` (VAL-12, ERR-10, CNF-4). A loader exception in TypeScript and PHP left `render` unchanged, and a filesystem loader reported a file that exists but cannot be read as missing in Go, Rust and PHP and as an exception in TypeScript. The Go `Loader.Load` now returns `(LoadResult, bool, error)` and the Rust `Loader::load` returns `Result<Option<Loaded>, String>`. JSON text that is not one JSON document was a usage error in the Go and Rust command line programs, an uncaught exception in the PHP command line program and `E_DATA_UNSUPPORTED_TYPE` in the PHP extension; Go and Rust also reported a number or depth error after an earlier syntax error. `JsonSyntaxError` is removed from TypeScript and PHP.
- Aligned the specification with the parsers for two tag starts: `{ :x = 1}` is an assignment (LEX-6, GRM-1, GRM-5) and `{@item = items}` is a loop tag (LEX-5), as every parser, the formatter and the VS Code grammar already accepted. New conformance cases pin both forms and the `{@media ...}` text.
- Fixed two generated backend defects that the host value fixture exposed: the TypeScript backend did not compile a map literal whose values have different types, and the Go backend passed the nested list and map values of a list literal to functions as null (case `expr/mixed-literal-values`). The Rust backend no longer emits an unused mutable binding for an empty list literal.
- Made typed generated programs pass canonical values to runtime operations (docs/spec/compiler.md): a typed record reaches a function argument, an operator operand and an echo as a map of its declared fields, also inside a typed list or map. The TypeScript and PHP backends passed their record types, so a render that passed a record to a function failed with `E_INTERNAL`, and the Go backend passed a record as a native object. `make generated-native-check` compiles the new fixture `tests/fixtures/typed-values` with a typed manifest in every backend and compares the output with the AST program.
- Added the AST presence check of CNF-15 to `scripts/check-schema.mjs` with a mutation test, so a case without its `expected.ast.json` fails `make docs-check` and `make check`.
- Formatted the PHP extension and the Rust showcase adapter with rustfmt and allowed dead code only for the generated modules that the adapter includes. `make lint` checks the formatting of the extension and the adapter and compiles the adapter with warnings as errors; `make test-ext` runs clippy with warnings as errors on the extension.
- Verification: `make check` passed (exit status 0), including 1220 of 1220 AST conformance checks over 244 cases in five implementations, 244 of 244 generated cases in each of TypeScript, Go, Rust and PHP, 3624 delimiter matrix checks, the generated native call, host value and typed value checks, 748 TypeScript, 608 PHP and 293 extension tests and the Go and Rust suites. `make dependency-audit` and `make dependency-policy-check` passed. Each new language test failed on the previous code.
- Updated eslint to 10.11.0, typescript-eslint to 8.71.0 and `@types/node` to 26.6.3, and updated brace-expansion to 5.0.12 and fast-uri to 3.1.8, which fix the advisories that `npm audit` reported. TypeScript stays at 6.0.3 because typescript-eslint 8.71.0 accepts `>=4.8.4 <6.1.0`, and esbuild stays at 0.27.2, as recorded in `config/dependency-policy.json`.
- Made `make conformance`, `make delimiter-matrix` and `make parity` build every implementation first. They ran the Go and Rust command line programs and the PHP extension from the last build, so after a source change they tested old binaries: 29 failures came only from stale builds, and a stale build could equally hide a failure.
- Added the formatter package `@polyspec/template-format` with `format()` and the command `template-fmt`. The formatter normalizes whitespace inside tags by the style in `docs/operations/editor-tools.md`, keeps text outside tags, comments, directives and multi-line tags, and returns a result only when the formatted AST equals the source AST without spans. The command prints, rewrites (`--write`) or checks (`--check`, exit status 1) files and directories, reads standard input, and reports a file that does not parse with its position and exit status 2.
- Added the VS Code extension `polyspec-template` for `.tpl` files: a TextMate grammar that includes HTML and injects template tags into text, attribute values, CSS, JavaScript and HTML comments with a scope per tag kind and per expression token, a language configuration with `{* *}` comments and delimiter brackets, and document and range formatting that calls `@polyspec/template-format`. The `.vsix` bundles the formatter and the parser for the Node.js 24 runtime of VS Code 1.138 and later. The extension declares support for untrusted and virtual workspaces, because VS Code disables an extension without that declaration, including its grammar, in Restricted Mode. An integration test installs the `.vsix` into VS Code 1.138.0, opens an untrusted folder with workspace trust enabled, and checks the language, activation, the scopes of a tag inside an attribute value through `_workbench.captureSyntaxTokens` and formatting through `vscode.executeFormatDocumentProvider`; a second run without `capabilities` requires VS Code to disable the extension.
- Added parse diagnostics and tag structure to the VS Code extension. `templateStructure()` of `@polyspec/template-format` returns the parse error with string positions or the block constructs taken from the parser's tag ranges; a test matches the constructs to the AST spans of every conformance case. The extension publishes the parse error with its code at the parser position, highlights the tags of one construct, folds multi-line constructs and provides Go to Matching Template Tag (`Cmd+Alt+\` on macOS, `Ctrl+Alt+\` on Windows and Linux). It does not check HTML element balance across template branches.
- Added the Makefile targets `build-format`, `test-format`, `format-check`, `format-external-check`, `install-cli`, `build-vscode`, `test-vscode`, `test-vscode-integration`, `vscode-package` and `vscode-install`, added `test-format`, `format-check`, `test-vscode` and `test-vscode-integration` to `make check`, and registered `template-format` and `template-vscode` in `contracts/features.json`. Refreshed the JavaScript generated artifact manifests, whose compiler digest includes `package-lock.json`.
- Verification: `make check` passed, including 583 formatter, invariant, structure and CLI tests, 5 grammar test files, 12 extension unit tests and the VS Code 1.138.0 integration runs (12 of 12 checks with the installed extension in an untrusted workspace, 2 of 2 checks without `capabilities`). `make format-external-check` passed on an external template tree of 10 files.
- Added the internal error `E_INTERNAL` (ERR-12, ERR-13). A method handler of the PHP extension cannot unwind into PHP, so every Rust panic aborted the PHP process, for example `date('2024-01-01T12:34:5é', 'Y')` and an array key that is not valid UTF-8. Every public parse, prepare and render operation of the four runtimes, of generated programs and of every extension method now reports a panic or a language runtime error as `E_INTERNAL`; a host callback that reports a failure through the error mechanism of its language stays `E_RUNTIME_HOST_FUNCTION`.
- Fixed the Rust date parser, which sliced the time field by bytes and panicked when a multi-byte character ended inside it (case `functions/date-multibyte-time-error`). A search of every byte-index slice of text in the Rust crate and the extension found no other slice that can split a character; the date offset parser now uses checked slices as well.
- Made the number rule of host binding depend only on the value (VAL-2): a finite number whose magnitude is greater than 2^53 − 1 is `E_DATA_NUMBER_RANGE` whether it is an integer, a float, a JSON integer literal or a JSON literal with an exponent. `bind`, `parseJson` and their Go, Rust, PHP and extension equivalents apply the rule alike. The case `functions/json-escaping` reads `1e21` from a template literal instead of from data.
- Required valid Unicode text for map keys (VAL-12 to VAL-17): array keys, property names, Go map keys and define ids that are not valid UTF-8, JavaScript strings and keys with an unpaired surrogate, and JSON `\u` escapes that leave a surrogate unpaired are `E_DATA_INVALID_UTF8`. A lone surrogate escape in a template string literal produces U+FFFD in every parser (EXP-3); the TypeScript parser kept the surrogate.
- Added a nesting depth limit of 64 for every template value (VAL-20, `E_DATA_DEPTH`). Binding and JSON parsing stop at the 65th level, so cyclic host structures and very deep data no longer overflow the stack or exhaust memory, and a list or map literal that would exceed the limit is `E_RUNTIME_LIMIT`.
- Defined native object access (VAL-18, VAL-19, EXP-19) and aligned every implementation: visibility comes from the declarations and not from the class scope of the caller, `__get` and `__call` are not consulted, `o['name']` reads the same field as `o.name`, a field or result that cannot be bound fails with its data code at the expression, a native object passed to a host function, a class function or a method arrives as the original host object (`Value::downcast_object` in Rust), closures and resources are rejected, a `stdClass` subclass is a map through `instanceof`, `JsonSerializable` is tried first, and exception messages of host code are kept. `TemplateObject::member` returns a `Result`, and Rust host functions return `HostError`.
- Made generated programs exact and isolated: every backend writes exact string literals, so control characters and `$` render as in the AST program (case `text/control-characters`); the PHP backend requires a namespace (`--php-namespace`, `phpNamespace`) and two generated PHP programs load in one process; native objects reach includes, block arguments and definition data in Go and Rust generated programs; generated Rust converts values without JSON; a local assigned with another type in a loop or branch takes the merged type; generated TypeScript, Go and PHP report binding failures as template errors.
- Verification: `make check` passed, including 235 conformance cases in four AST runtimes, the extension and four generated modes, the generated native-call check and the extension suite. Each new language test failed on the previous code: the new extension tests aborted the PHP process or failed, and the new PHP runtime tests failed for numbers, keys, closures, resources, depth and messages.
- Completed the PHP value binding of the PHP extension (VAL-14, RT-60). The extension rejected every PHP object, including an empty `stdClass`, which is the only PHP form of an empty map, and it had no `registerClass`. It now binds `stdClass` as a map and a `JsonSerializable` object as its value, retains other objects with their public properties and methods, and registers logical class functions. Rendering passes values to the engine without JSON, so objects keep their instances.
- Fixed HTML escaping in the Rust implementation, which the PHP extension also uses. After the first character that needs a reference, it copied the text byte by byte and panicked on the first multi-byte character, so `<제목>` aborted the Rust CLI and the PHP process. It now copies the text between replaced characters. The conformance case `echo/html-escape-multibyte` reproduces the failure in every language.
- Added a manifest-declared language test matrix that requires equal semantic coverage for TypeScript, Go, Rust and PHP in both AST and generated modes. Added mutation checks for missing language support, test paths and compiler modes, and included the matrix in the default check.
- Corrected the expression and guide documents to describe the implemented member-call and logical class-call syntax. The documents no longer state that method calls are unavailable or that generated native calls are partial.
- Extended the function contract with `object.method(args...)` and `Class::function(args...)` syntax and opened Wave 8 for native instance binding and execution. The parser and AST support these nodes; runtime execution remains partial.
- Added `MemberCall` and `ClassCall` nodes and aligned TypeScript, Go, Rust and PHP expression parsers on `object.method(args...)` and `Class::function(args...)`. Runtime object binding and execution remain partial until the next implementation stage.
